# Migrating from a legacy design system to Design System Tokens

Use this guide to map each legacy token to the right Design System token.
Focus on semantic meaning, not raw values.
This page is a migration reference, not a rollout plan.

## Design System Token Architecture

Tokens are the shared language between design and engineering. Everyone on the team (designers and engineers alike) must use this language. It replaces hard-coded hex values and raw numbers with names that carry meaning across platforms. The naming framework below makes it straightforward to read and write tokens correctly.

The system has three layers, and only one of them is yours to use in product code:

- **Foundation**: raw colour values, font families, font sizes, font weights, line heights, and dimension units. The source material for the entire system. Internal only.
- **Palette**: colour-only. Named ramps generated from foundation colour values. Semantic colour tokens alias palette steps; no other token type uses this layer, and neither do components directly. Internal only.
- **Semantic**: tokens named by UI purpose, not by value. They cover colour, typography, spacing, and radius. These are what you use in product code.

Use semantic tokens. They describe UI purpose, not raw values, so when brand decisions change underneath, your code stays intact.

### Token Architecture Framework

<!-- figma:architecture -->

### Semantic Token Naming

Tokens follow a `type.sentiment.role` naming pattern, with an optional trailing segment (`.state`, `.scale`, or `.hierarchy`) when the token varies by interaction, size, or prominence. Each segment describes purpose, not value.

<!-- figma:token-naming-anatomy -->

| Segment       | What it means                                           | Example                              |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
| **type**      | The property category                                   | `colour`, `typo`, `spacing`          |
| **sentiment** | The UX grouping                                         | `interactive`, `status`, `editorial` |
| **role**      | What the token targets                                  | `background`, `label`, `border`      |
| **state**     | Interaction variant: interactive tokens only            | `default`, `hover`, `pressed`        |
| **scale**     | Size variant: only when the token has multiple sizes    | `S`, `M`, `L`, `XL`                  |
| **hierarchy** | Prominence rank: only when the token has multiple tiers | `primary`, `secondary`, `tertiary`   |

Examples in code:

- `colour.interactive.background.default`: primary interactive fill at rest
- `utility.body.regular.medium`: standard body text for running copy
- `spacing.05`: 8px spacing step for component padding

In Figma variables, tokens carry a `ds.` prefix, for example `ds.colour.interactive.background.default`. The code-side path omits it.

This replaces the legacy system's direct value coupling (for example `Foundations.blue070` or `sizing.sizing060`) with names that encode usage, not values.

### Colour Ramps

Design System uses 20-step ramps (`50` to `1000`). the legacy system uses 10 steps (`010` to `100`).
**Step numbers do not correspond.** Select by semantic intent, not numeric proximity.
One foundation hex change regenerates both light and dark ramps automatically.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> In dark mode, neutral ramps run in reverse.
> `neutral.50` is black in dark mode. `neutral.1000` is white.
> Always verify hex values using the `hex_lookup` MCP tool before finalising neutral mappings.
> Do not match by step number alone.

## Platform Outputs

`packages/tokens/src/tokens.json` is the single source of truth for the design system team, and we transform it into consumer-ready outputs such as CSS, Android, iOS, SCSS, and Figma variables.

<!-- figma:platform-outputs -->

## Colour

Semantics gives you the destination, but ramp thinking guides selection. Treat colour migration as intent migration, not hue migration.

### Design System Anchored Reference

This section defines architecture-level semantic token selection. It is intentionally DS-first and semantic-first, so your implementation can stay stable as colour ramps evolve.

```ts
// Before: direct legacy foundation coupling
color: tokens.Foundations.blue070;

// After: Design System semantic intent
color: tokens['interactive.link.primary.text.default'];
```

### Legacy Anchored Reference

Resolved intent mappings for every legacy token referenced in the semantic mapping table above.
Each row shows the closest DS semantic token match to use as a bridge-planning aid.

> [!NOTE]
> **H**: very close intent match. **M**: related intent, scope differs. **L**: distant but usable bridge. **Orphan**: no credible semantic equivalent.
> Score colours follow the DS `ramp.sequential` data-visualisation ramp (green -> red).
